# Combined Investment Research System: Detailed Meaty End-to-End Goal

Date: 2026-09-17  
Repository: `annual-report-research`

## The goal in one sentence

Build a source-grounded investment research system that starts with social
behavior and macro forces, identifies the industries and companies exposed to
those forces, verifies the real operating economics through primary filings,
translates the business story into cash flows and valuation, tests liquidity
and balance-sheet durability, and—where the evidence permits—traces capital
through named financial wrappers, borrowers, projects, assets, receipts,
repayments, and returns.

In plain English:

> What is changing in society and the economy, who controls the valuable
> workflow or bottleneck, who pays for the system, who carries the hardest
> costs, how does reported activity become owner cash, what price already
> assumes, and what document would prove the thesis wrong?

## What this system is meant to produce

The output is not a collection of disconnected theme essays, company notes,
or valuation screens. It is a connected evidence chain:

```text
social behavior and cultural change
        -> macro, liquidity, and policy forces
        -> industry structure and economic control points
        -> private-company and growth-company discovery
        -> public-company filing evidence
        -> operating model and burden map
        -> reported earnings to owner-cash bridge
        -> Damodaran valuation and reinvestment analysis
        -> Lyn Alden durability and liquidity tests
        -> capital-source, wrapper, borrower, and asset tracing
        -> cash receipt, repayment, waterfall, and return evidence
        -> valuation conclusion, confidence level, and thesis breaker
```

Each stage should narrow uncertainty. A broad force can generate a research
question, but it cannot by itself prove a company claim. A company filing can
prove a reported fact, but it cannot by itself prove that the market price is
attractive. A valuation can show what must happen, but it cannot prove that
the required growth will occur. A capital-flow map can show who funds a
system, but it is not complete until the destination, use, cash return, and
legal availability are evidenced.

## The source architecture

The system should preserve the different jobs performed by each source family.
Sources are connected, but they are not interchangeable.

For a reader-facing review of the work already produced, start with the
[current end-to-end synthesis](combined-investment-research-current-synthesis.md),
then use the [reviewer's guide](combined-investment-research-reviewers-guide.md)
and its [five-theme status map](data/combined-investment-research-theme-status-map-2026-09-16.csv).
These entry points expose the representative writeups, connected pilots,
current proof grades, source-family handoffs, and decisive open question for
each lane. They are navigation aids, not substitutes for the underlying
filing ledgers or evidence gates.

The [evidence-chain handoff matrix](combined-investment-research-evidence-chain-handoff-matrix-2026-09-16.md)
is the structured control for the full path from force through operating model,
QoE, owner cash, valuation, macro/liquidity, capital flow, and thesis breaker.
It preserves the pilot-specific bottleneck instead of collapsing unlike proof
grades into a single investment ranking.

| Source layer | Primary question | Safe use | What it cannot prove alone |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Social research | What are people doing, valuing, fearing, adopting, or abandoning? | Early signals, consumer behavior, trust, identity, repeat use, cultural change | Population prevalence, causality, market size, or company economics without validation |
| Inc. 5000 analysis | Which private companies are growing around a specific customer pain or force? | Discovery of emerging business models, pain points, sectors, and growth hypotheses | Durable profits, capital efficiency, public-company comparability, or verified cash returns without deeper evidence |
| IBIS Industries | Which industries and forces are changing together? | Sector map, macro force map, industry structure, adjacency discovery, gap-finding | Exact company-period facts or verified earnings claims |
| Strategy and theme research | How does a force reshape institutions, consumers, ownership, or operating systems? | Long-form causal interpretation and cross-sector hypotheses | Filing-level measurement or valuation precision |
| Annual reports, 10-Ks, 10-Qs, and official IR | What did a named company report, for what period, and under what accounting rules? | Primary company facts, segment economics, liabilities, capital spending, cash flow, incentives, and management claims | Future value, causal certainty, or proof that adjusted metrics equal owner cash |
| Earnings calls and transcripts | What does management say is changing and what will be tested next? | Timing, operational explanation, guidance, risks, and management incentives | Independent verification of management's claims |
| Damodaran method library | What cash flows, reinvestment, risk, and terminal assumptions justify value? | Narrative-to-numbers, DCF, comparable valuation, lifecycle, cost of capital, growth quality, and capital allocation | Evidence that the assumptions are true |
| Lyn Alden-style macro/liquidity analysis | How do money, debt, inflation, rates, policy, and funding conditions affect durability? | Liquidity, nominal claims, debt burden, monetary regime, affordability, and funding stress | Company-specific operating proof or precise valuation by itself |
| Investments article and hard-gate bridge | How do article-derived hypotheses, Lyn Alden/Damodaran workflows, and metric pass/block decisions enter the filing research queue? | Hypothesis extraction, exact metric requests, article-to-fundamental alignment, and explicit hard gates | Article stance, alignment score, or hard-gate status cannot replace primary filing, denominator, cash, or falsifier evidence |
| Capital-flow and legal-entity evidence | Who supplied the money, which wrapper routed it, who received it, and what came back? | Named borrowers, assets, projects, insurance entities, credit vehicles, collateral, receipts, waterfalls, and returns | A complete cash loop when a decisive document is missing |

## Cross-pilot themes and reading lens

The three pilots are intentionally different, but they are tested through the
same deeper themes:

1. **Control point versus burden carrier.** Identify who owns the scarce asset,
   customer relationship, workflow, or balance-sheet access—and who actually
   carries operating, regulatory, financing, policyholder, labor, or
   reinvestment burden.
2. **Reported activity versus owner cash.** Treat revenue, segment earnings,
   operating cash, consolidated cash, statutory cash, and common-owner cash as
   separate objects. Every bridge must identify the legal entity, period,
   claims senior to the owner, and any temporary support.
3. **Quality of earnings and financial shenanigans.** Use working-capital
   timing, supplier finance, capitalization, stock compensation, revenue
   recognition, related-party flows, and unusual margins as falsifiable
   diagnostics. Sloan- and Schilit-style flags are prompts for reconciliation;
   they are not fraud findings, and a composite Beneish score is not assembled
   until the accounting perimeter is comparable.
4. **Capital stack and waterfall.** A contractual facility, reserve, regulatory
   capacity, or named asset is not the same as funded cash. The chain must run
   from source to borrower or operator, use of proceeds, senior claims,
   repayment, receipt, and residual return.
5. **Expectation versus proof.** Damodaran-style valuation shows what must be
   true; Lyn Alden-style liquidity analysis tests durability under macro and
   funding stress; primary filings determine what is actually observed. A
   thesis breaker is activated only when the same-entity, same-period
   cash-or-claim test is met.

These themes are the common vocabulary connecting the social, Inc. 5000, IBIS,
annual-report, QoE, valuation, capital-flow, and thesis-breaker layers. They
also explain why the current work can support qualified hypotheses without
claiming a final cross-sector ranking.

## The research chain

### 1. Detect the force

Start with observed social, cultural, demographic, technological, industrial,
financial, regulatory, or geopolitical change. State the observation without
turning it into a conclusion.

Examples include aging demand, affordability pressure, AI infrastructure,
reshoring, labor scarcity, healthcare workflow strain, trust and verification,
private-credit expansion, power scarcity, recurring consumption, or the shift
from ownership to access.

Required output:

- a plain-English description of the force;
- the time horizon and geography;
- the observed behavior or institutional change;
- the parties helped and burdened;
- the uncertainty and alternative explanations; and
- the specific economic question worth testing.

### 2. Map the industry system

Use the IBIS and strategy layers to map the industry around the force. Identify
the customer, supplier, intermediary, platform, asset owner, labor provider,
regulator, lender, insurer, and end payer.

The key question is not simply which sector is growing. It is:

> Where is the scarce control point, and who captures the economics while
> another party funds or performs the difficult work?

Required output:

- industry and adjacent-industry map;
- control point or bottleneck;
- customer decision and payment chain;
- operating and financing dependencies;
- likely burden carriers;
- companies to investigate; and
- the evidence gaps that prevent a stronger conclusion.

### 3. Generate and rank companies

Use Inc. 5000 data, public-company rosters, industry databases, and existing
company packets to identify candidates. Treat private-company growth as a
discovery signal, not as proof of quality.

Rank candidates using several lenses:

- size plus growth, not growth percentage alone;
- revenue per employee or another relevant productivity measure;
- customer pain and willingness to pay;
- control of a repeated, qualified, trusted, or scarce workflow;
- durability through a full cycle;
- capital intensity and financing dependence;
- evidence availability; and
- valuation relevance.

Each candidate should have a hypothesis in the form:

`what it sells -> whose pain it solves -> who pays -> why now -> what could make the growth durable -> what could break it`

### 4. Build the company evidence packet

For each priority public company, collect and preserve the annual report,
latest three relevant quarters, earnings release, earnings call, investor
materials, source ledger, and important regulatory or legal documents.

Every material fact must identify:

- company and legal entity;
- document and source route;
- reporting period or as-of date;
- exact metric or quoted claim;
- unit and denominator;
- whether it is reported, calculated, inferred, or unresolved; and
- the next filing or document that could change it.

The packet must separate management language from independently visible
financial and operating evidence.

### 5. Identify the economic control point

Explain what the customer is actually buying and why the company is difficult
to replace. Possible control points include:

- a trusted brand or recurring consumer occasion;
- an installed base and consumable pull-through;
- a qualified clinical or industrial workflow;
- a distribution route, inventory position, or procurement interface;
- a network, standard, exchange, or payment rail;
- regulated infrastructure or scarce physical capacity;
- a software control layer or data workflow; or
- a financing, insurance, or balance-sheet relationship.

Then identify who bears the burden of labor, inventory, facilities, quality,
regulation, warranty, customer credit, property renewal, debt, or dilution.

### 6. Translate the narrative into an operating model

Convert the company story into measurable drivers. The model must show how the
business works before discussing whether it is attractive.

Examples:

- customers or transactions × revenue per transaction;
- units × price × mix;
- installed base × utilization × recurring consumables;
- subscribers × retention × price × service cost;
- backlog × conversion × margin × working-capital timing;
- assets × yield × funding cost × losses;
- rate base × allowed return × recovery timing; or
- AUM × fee rate × flows × performance income × required capital.

The operating denominator must be appropriate to the business. Revenue,
orders, AUM, backlog, RPO, members, rooms, treatments, or adjusted EBITDA are
activity or management measures—not automatically owner cash.

### 7. Reconcile earnings to owner cash

Build a visible bridge from reported earnings to cash available to common
owners. At minimum, test:

```text
reported earnings or operating metric
  -> operating cash flow
  -> working-capital timing
  -> maintenance and growth capital spending
  -> capitalized operating costs
  -> acquisitions and integration costs
  -> recurring restructuring or “one-time” items
  -> debt service and required balance-sheet funding
  -> minority interests, preferred claims, leases, and legal obligations
  -> stock compensation and diluted share count
  -> owner-cash or common-owner residual
```

The definition must change by sector. Banks, insurers, REITs, utilities,
private-credit platforms, and asset managers require different denominators.
The analysis must state what is included, excluded, and still unknown.

### 8. Apply the Damodaran valuation layer

Use Damodaran's methods to force the story into explicit assumptions:

- expected revenue and margin path;
- reinvestment required to produce growth;
- incremental return on invested capital;
- lifecycle and competitive transition;
- cost of equity and cost of capital;
- debt-equity and payout choices;
- terminal growth and terminal economics;
- comparable-company limitations; and
- the difference between market price and intrinsic value.

The valuation must state what the current price implies. It should show the
growth, margin, reinvestment, duration, or multiple assumptions that must be
true for the investment to work.

No valuation row should imply precision when the market snapshot, cash bridge,
share count, or denominator is incomplete.

### 9. Apply the Lyn Alden macro and liquidity layer

Test whether the company can remain durable under changes in:

- inflation and input costs;
- wages and labor availability;
- household affordability;
- interest rates and refinancing costs;
- money supply, credit availability, and liquidity;
- government deficits, regulation, and policy transfers;
- currency and commodity exposure;
- long-duration nominal liabilities;
- collateral values and funding access; and
- the difference between accounting solvency and cash survival.

This layer must be specific to the company. It should explain the transmission
mechanism from macro condition to customer demand, margin, funding, valuation,
or legal solvency.

### 10. Trace capital flows where the question requires it

For selected cases, follow the chain:

```text
capital source
  -> platform or router
  -> legal wrapper
  -> instrument
  -> named borrower, issuer, project, asset, or collateral pool
  -> use of proceeds
  -> operating output or asset ownership
  -> receipt, billing, settlement, repayment, income, or sale proceeds
  -> waterfall, collateral availability, or legal-entity distribution
  -> return, spread, yield, IRR, ROIC, or common-owner residual
```

Capital sources may include retirement savers, insurance liabilities, ETF
holders, institutions, private wealth, banks, public debt buyers, BDC
investors, or operating cash.

The system must distinguish:

1. **Map evidence** — the platform, lane, or destination is visible.
2. **Bridge evidence** — a named recipient, asset, project, or instrument is
   visible.
3. **Named-cash hold** — most of the route is visible, but one decisive
   receipt, waterfall, legal schedule, or return document is missing.
4. **Full named-cash proof** — the source, wrapper, destination, use, cash
   return, legal route, and return are joined without guessing.

Useful proof families include receipts and billing, settlement ledgers, debt
waterfalls, legal-entity schedules, collateral certificates, borrowing-base
reports, statutory insurance schedules, rate orders, trustee reports, covenant
tables, and return models.

### 11. Write the conclusion and falsifier

Every company or cohort conclusion must answer:

1. What does the company control?
2. What does the customer pay for?
3. Which party performs the labor or funds the physical system?
4. What is the correct operating denominator?
5. How does the preferred metric reconcile to GAAP and cash?
6. What liabilities, reinvestment, financing, acquisition, or dilution claims
   remain against the owner?
7. What does the market price assume?
8. What is durable, what is cyclical, and what is merely management narrative?
9. What is the confidence level: proven, qualified, partial, or unresolved?
10. What exact filing or document would weaken or disprove the thesis?

## Required deliverables

The mature system should contain:

- a social and macro force atlas;
- an IBIS-style industry and adjacency map;
- an Inc. 5000 growth-company discovery layer;
- company packets and source ledgers;
- deep company dossiers;
- first-principles cohort essays;
- cross-sector comparisons;
- a Damodaran method and valuation registry;
- Lyn Alden macro/liquidity routes;
- an article-derived hypothesis and hard-gate bridge to the filing queue;
- owner-cash and denominator-specific valuation tables;
- a capital-source and destination map;
- named-cash proof tables for selected cases;
- evidence-grade thesis-breaker registers;
- reproducible source routes;
- a reader-facing site that distinguishes depth and confidence; and
- a completion audit that prevents broad coverage from being mistaken for
  equally deep proof.

## Definition of done

The project is done only when a skeptical reader can move from a broad force
to a company conclusion without having to trust an unstated leap:

1. The originating social, macro, or industry observation is named and dated.
2. The affected industry and control point are explained.
3. The candidate company or cohort is selected for a stated reason.
4. The primary filing evidence is locally traceable.
5. The operating model identifies who pays and who bears the burden.
6. Reported performance is reconciled to the correct cash denominator.
7. Reinvestment, financing, liabilities, acquisitions, and dilution are
   visible.
8. Damodaran assumptions convert the narrative into a valuation range.
9. Lyn Alden-style macro and liquidity risks are connected to specific company
   mechanisms.
10. Capital-flow claims are labeled by proof level and do not overclaim.
11. The price-implied expectation is explicit.
12. The conclusion ends with a measurable filing-based thesis breaker.
13. Every important claim has a reproducible source path.

The final standard is not “we found an interesting story.” It is:

> We can explain what changed, who controls the economics, who funds the
> burden, how the business becomes cash, what the market assumes, and which
> primary document would prove us wrong.

## Immediate execution priority

The current work should deepen selected cases instead of expanding the roster
without limit:

1. Choose one social/industry force with a visible company cohort.
2. Choose one public-company flagship and one adjacent company or private-
   company discovery case.
3. Complete the operating, owner-cash, Damodaran, and Lyn Alden tests.
4. Continue the Wheaton–Antamina capital-flow case toward delivery-level
   named-cash proof, then select a second capital-flow case—preferably
   Apollo/Athene or Ares/Frontline—for comparison.
5. Record every missing document and the exact upgrade test.
6. Publish the cohort explanation only after the company evidence supports it.
7. Mark the lane proven, qualified, partial, or unresolved.

The project should stop broadening a lane when its next useful contribution is
not another theme or company name but a receipt, legal schedule, cash bridge,
valuation input, or filing-based falsifier.

The [next-cycle candidate expansion handoff](combined-investment-research-next-cycle-candidate-expansion-2026-09-16.md)
implements that rule after the current pilots. It sequences power-grid
customer cash, insurance statutory named-asset income, and asset-backed
collateral/borrowing-base work, each with an admission protocol, QoE and
denominator gate, and promotion breaker. These are expansion lanes, not a
claim that the candidates are attractive or that CA-06 is complete.
The power-grid lane is already active in the repository: its FPL Distribution
Inspection receipt chase is the first concrete next-cycle test, with AEP and
Duke as comparison routes.

## Current execution order — move-on routing, 2026-09-17

The public-source refreshes have now been used to separate actionable work from
lanes whose next decisive document is not publicly available. The system should
not spend another cycle repeating a searched-negative query. The next work is
sequenced by decision value and source-actionability:

1. **Q-03 and CA-06/Q-04–Q-06:** work the next joinable denominator or
   cash-flow object—closing funds-flow, facility-specific repayment, matched
   tax/lease cash, maintenance-capital allocation, recurring working capital,
   or attached-service collection and cost allocation.
2. **Q-07–Q-09:** pursue only a dated AGM receipt/elimination ledger, named
   custodian/trustee settlement, liability-cost join, executed ARI settlement,
   borrower receipt, or true-up document; consolidated proxies remain parked.
3. **Q-12/Q-13:** use the scheduled MF1 publication window and any populated
   borrowing-base certificate, NOLV/reserve schedule, or source-to-purchase
   record as the next statutory and asset-backed promotion tests.
4. **Q-10/Q-11:** refresh macro causality or regulatory recovery only when a
   repeated aligned observation, discovery response, workpaper, or true-up can
   change the proof grade.
5. **Q-01/Q-02:** keep the BHP-PMPA-only settlement object and reserve-backed
   curve as time-gated watch items. The current public perimeter is searched-
   negative or evidence-insufficient; do not repeat the same search without a
   new filing, delivery report, settlement statement, or reserve source.

This is a routing decision, not a conclusion that the parked claims are false.
It preserves the original end-to-end scope while directing effort toward the
next document capable of closing a denominator, receipt, repayment, or return
join.

This order preserves the original scope while preventing additional thematic
expansion from outrunning the unresolved cash, claim, and receipt gates.

The [next-execution handoff](combined-investment-research-next-execution-handoff-2026-09-17.md)
updates this routing after the latest workbench cycle. Q-03 now has a precise
metal-credit receipt target; CA-06/Q-04–Q-06 has a matched-period allocation
target; URI has a certificate/lifecycle target; private credit has a lender
allocation/cash-waterfall target; and the physical-capacity cohort has
company-specific project and collateral targets. The next useful contribution
must change one of those source-backed promotion gates rather than repeat a
summary or searched-negative query.

## First execution already started

The first integrated pilot is [Wheaton–Antamina](combined-investment-research-pilot-01-wheaton-antamina.md).
It was selected because the repository already contains a named capital
deployment, a named contractual asset, post-close production, combined
received-and-sold ounces, stream revenue, an operating-cash proxy, funding
context, and a precise list of missing receipt, waterfall, tax, and return
documents. Its current status is deliberately qualified rather than promoted
to full proof.

The second pilot is [Affordability, Substitution, and Retail Value](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-affordability-value.md).
It connects social-research signals and Inc. 5000 business-formation context
to a filing-backed TJX/Target/Walmart cohort. Its social and private-company
inputs remain directional; the annual-report layer carries the company and
cash conclusions.

The third pilot is [Apollo–Athene Origination to Common-Owner Cash](combined-investment-research-pilot-03-apollo-athene.md).
It is the second capital-flow case. It separates Apollo's fee-related
earnings, Athene's insurance-spread economics, principal-investing income, and
the origination/deployment funnel. The case is qualified because activity and
adjusted earnings are visible, while realized cash, regulated capital,
policyholder liabilities, credit losses, and HoldCo-to-common-owner cash are
not yet fully reconciled.

The structured pilot artifacts are checked by
[`verify-combined-investment-pilots.py`](../../scripts/verify-combined-investment-pilots.py),
which currently validates all three evidence ledgers, all source-artifact paths,
the TJX missing-valuation boundary, the retail owner-cash bridge, and the
Target/Walmart scenario math.

## Current execution state — 2026-09-16

The first pass has now produced three integrated pilots, three evidence
ledgers, three valuation workbenches, a retail owner-cash bridge, and a repository
verifier. The Wheaton pilot
received a material primary-source upgrade: Wheaton's closing disclosure and
BHP's FY2026 financing cash-flow disclosure now corroborate the approximately
`4.300B USD` upfront payment/proceeds loop. This upgrades the case from
one-sided payment evidence to a two-sided upfront cash confirmation.

That upgrade does not close the investment-return question. The next required
evidence is still the BHP-PMPA-only delivery and settlement trail, followed by
Antamina-specific tax and interest allocation, lender repayment/waterfall
evidence, a reserve-backed delivery curve, and a source-backed IRR/NPV. The
public-source boundary is recorded in the pilot's
[primary-source upgrade memo](capital-flow-wheaton-antamina-public-primary-source-upgrade-2026-09-15.md).

The Apollo–Athene pilot adds the comparison case required by the capital-flow
lane: fee-related earnings, insurance spread, principal income, origination,
deployment, and a named Broadcom financing context are all visible, but the
flow to Apollo common-owner cash remains qualified until realized credit,
regulated-capital, liability, NCI, tax, financing, and dilution evidence is
joined.

The Q2 2026 Apollo 10-Q has now supplied the first hard attribution layer:
`21.006B USD` Apollo stockholders' equity, `20.476B USD` noncontrolling
interests, `1.398B USD` mandatory convertible preferred stock, `452.566B USD`
total liabilities, `4.501B USD` six-month consolidated operating cash, and
`478M USD` of six-month equity-based compensation plus `1.1B USD` unrecognized
RSU cost. The [primary-source upgrade memo](capital-flow-apollo-athene-primary-source-upgrade-2026-09-15.md)
records the exact boundaries: consolidated cash is not fee-platform cash,
regulated and restricted cash is not freely distributable, and Apollo Capital
Markets Partnership commitments are not the same as Apollo-funded capital.

The structured system now contains `225` evidence gates across the three pilots;
the verifier checks all three valuation workbenches and the Apollo
primary-source claim stack.

The latest Apollo–Athene parent-receipt refresh adds a legal-availability
boundary below AHL: AHL names subsidiary dividends, capital-market issuance,
and intercompany borrowing as primary cash sources, but says it does not
currently plan for U.S. insurance subsidiaries to pay dividends to their
parents; Bermuda subsidiary distributions are constrained by statute,
regulation, surplus, and desired financial-strength ratings. This sharpens the
upstream route without promoting the `$110M` H1 AHL distribution into an AGM
receipt or common-owner cash.

The retail attached-service denominator is now explicitly reconciled: Walmart's
frontier uses a `$3.855B` segment subtotal, while the consolidated filing
reports `$3.904B`; the `$49M` difference is carried as an unreconciled
perimeter item in the [Walmart attached-income denominator reconciliation](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-walmart-attached-income-denominator-reconciliation-2026-09-16.md).
Neither denominator is promoted to attached-service cash or normalized owner
cash.

The Apollo capital-flow case now separates the named Broadcom/Anthropic
wrapper from its fee-recognition timing. The Q2 filing identifies the
Apollo-managed WHCO purchase obligation and Athene's limited default
guarantee; the Q2 earnings transcript says the `$35B` financing is expected to
draw over multiple quarters and the related fee to be recognized as drawn,
weighted toward Q4 2026 and Q1–Q3 2027. These are source-backed legal and
timing boundaries, not proof of lender proceeds, fee collection, guarantee
utilization, or Apollo common-owner cash.

The latest controlled additions make two previously easy-to-confuse routes
explicit. Apollo's Q2 2026 10-Q reports `$799M` of fund distributions to the
Company in the performance-allocation rollforward, alongside a broader
`$2.040B` consolidated investment-fund/equity-method cash-flow line; a focused
PDF assertion confirms both observations and keeps them separate from
Athene's `$110M` H1 distribution-to-parent frontier. Retail gate AVP-051 now
joins TJX, Target, and Walmart inventory/payable balance changes to cash-flow
cross-checks, while preventing those balance movements from being subtracted
from operating cash a second time. Neither addition promotes normalized
common-owner cash. The TJX forward-capex gate also quantifies the FY2027
spending guide into approximately `$1.000B` renovations, `$992M`
offices/distribution/IT, and `$222M` new stores; this narrows forward burden
visibility while leaving the H1 maintenance-versus-growth split unresolved.

The latest attribution pass adds three important qualified boundaries without
promoting them to full cash proof: Athene's Q2 filing now exposes dated
distributions to its direct parent (`$32M` in Q2 and `$110M` in H1), a direct
AHL-to-AGM revolving-note route, and a buyer-side commercial-mortgage balance
that is directionally consistent with the ARI purchase; the new Q2 receipt
frontier quantifies the unresolved `$0M–$110M` AGM-attribution range without
promoting it to cash. The Apollo Q2 filing still does not join those
observations to an unrestricted AGM receipt or a common-owner residual. On the Wheaton side, BHP's FY2026 filing now supports
the legal metal-credit settlement mechanism, a `$41M` aggregate streaming-
liability settlement boundary, and a BHP-interest production/sales proxy, but
not BHP-only ounces, invoice allocation, or settled cash. Wheaton's Q2 MD&A
now separately confirms that the first BHP-PMPA deliveries were received
during Q2 2026; the new [first-delivery receipt boundary](capital-flow-wheaton-antamina-q2-first-delivery-receipt-boundary-2026-09-15.md)
keeps the reported `2.319M` produced and `2.063M` sold combined Antamina ounces separate from BHP-only
metal-credit quantity and cash. These distinctions are recorded in the
ledgers and next-evidence queue rather than silently treated as completed
capital-flow proof.
The Q2 financial statements add a same-period combined economics anchor:
Antamina stream revenue was `$150.549M`, profit was `$77.323M`, operating
cash flow was `$122.039M`, and stream assets were `$4.708B`. This improves the
period cash-proxy bridge while preserving the unresolved BHP-only settlement,
tax, financing, and common-owner allocation requirements.
The same filing's accounting policy defines the next join: precious-metal
credit revenue is recognized when credits are sold and control transfers,
while concentrate final settlement depends on recovered ounces, smelter
weights, assays, and the quotation period. The policy strengthens the receipt
test's object specification; it does not supply the BHP-only invoice or cash
entry.
The [Q-03 metal-credit receipt boundary](capital-flow-wheaton-antamina-q03-metal-credit-receipt-boundary-2026-09-17.md)
now makes that object explicit: BHP documents settlement through metal credits
with no physical delivery, so the next promotion request is a BHP-specific
credit issuance, credit-sale, receivable, and bank-receipt ledger. The combined
`2.319M` produced, `2.063M` sold, and `1.412M` undelivered payable-metal
figures remain unallocated between the BHP and legacy Glencore streams.
The authoritative WPM-054 ledger gate now also records the page-level
corroboration: Investor Day pages 37 and 41 identify the separate BHP and
Glencore PMPA routes, while page 47 presents the combined Antamina reserve
denominator. This strengthens the denominator control and still does not
convert the combined reserve table into BHP-only delivery or settlement cash.
The new profile-to-reserve consistency screen places the Investor Day `6.0M`
and `5.4M` forward profile beside the `59.13M` mechanical payable-ounce
ceiling: the first-ten-year profile sums to `57.0M`, or approximately `96.4%`
of that ceiling, leaving `2.13M` ounces under the stated-basis case. This
narrows the expectation surface but remains a sensitivity, not a reserve-backed
delivery curve or BHP-PMPA settlement ledger.

The macro/liquidity layer now also has a checked cross-pilot stress screen, a
historical 2020–2026 regime-validation panel, a same-period 2026 company/regime
join, and a current-regime validation memo:
retail temporary-support ranges, Antamina price/production/duration screens,
and Apollo spread, policyholder-liquidity, and upstream-receipt tests are
shown with explicit output or denominator-held status. The historical panel
improves regime consistency but remains below through-cycle causal proof.
The Q-10 follow-up now tests the 18-row retail bridge in three ways: pooled
real-wage/CPI buckets, within-company transitions, and lagged macro conditions.
The within-company comparison is evenly split between same-direction and
opposite-direction transitions, while the lagged comparison finds `7` same-
direction versus `8` opposite-direction usable transitions. These results are
useful negative evidence against a simple universal macro-to-cash rule; they
do not establish causality because company actions, mix, seasonality, policy,
and accounting changes remain incompletely controlled.

The valuation layer now has an explicit reverse-expectation bridge: Target and
Walmart market values are translated into required cash at the stated
multiples, Apollo's market value is translated into required FRE, SRE, or
principal/performance value under held-constant assumptions, and Wheaton's
consolidated market cap is explicitly marked non-identifiable for Antamina.
TJX has a bounded cash sensitivity documented, but its valuation workbench
remains `not-comparable` until the owner-cash gates are closed.

The current evidence system also preserves a Walmart Q2 FY2027 management-call
control-point memo and structured claim ledger. It connects management's
value, fulfillment, marketplace, advertising, membership, and tariff-refund
observations to explicit burden and next-test fields. This improves the
operating-mechanism layer while keeping attached-service cash and normalized
owner cash excluded until allocated cost, capital, tax, working-capital, and
common-owner evidence is available.

The current reader-facing state is consolidated in the [end-to-end synthesis](combined-investment-research-current-synthesis.md), which audits each goal requirement as proven, partial, qualified, or unresolved and preserves the next source-backed upgrade for every active pilot. The retail lane now also has checked [owner-cash](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-retail-owner-cash-bridge.md), [burden-normalization](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-retail-burden-normalization.md), [normalized-cash screen](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-retail-normalized-cash-screen.md), [annual per-share cash](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-retail-annual-per-share-cash.md), [TJX temporary-support](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-tjx-h1-temporary-support.md), and [capex-classification boundary](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-retail-capex-classification.md) artifacts: TJX, Target, and Walmart have annual reported OCF-less-property bridges, TJX now has an H1 FY2027 bridge and explicit temporary-support sensitivity, Walmart now has exact H1 FY2027 capex categories, and maintenance-versus-growth amounts remain explicitly unresolved where filings do not quantify them. Pilot 01 also has a [BHP settlement-boundary upgrade](capital-flow-wheaton-antamina-bhp-fy2026-settlement-boundary-upgrade-2026-09-15.md) and a [Q2 first-delivery receipt boundary](capital-flow-wheaton-antamina-q2-first-delivery-receipt-boundary-2026-09-15.md): first BHP-PMPA deliveries are now confirmed, but only the combined Antamina quantity is disclosed. Pilot 03 now has a [related-party return bridge](combined-investment-research-pilot-03-apollo-related-party-return-bridge.md) and a [named-asset route map](combined-investment-research-pilot-03-apollo-named-asset-return-routes.md) that preserve the Athene-to-Apollo cash-access boundary while identifying Concord, AMAPS 1, and AP Aristotle as concrete next proof routes.

The reader-facing deliverable also has a dated [Chromium browser-review record](combined-investment-research-reader-browser-review-2026-09-16.md). The runtime suite exercises the integrated investment-research path, evidence-depth and confidence language, Target's Q2 call memo, Apollo's fee-rollforward boundary, the three Q-10 retail diagnostics, article routes, mobile layout, search, filters, and trend handoffs. This verifies presentation and navigation; it does not upgrade the underlying investment evidence.

The latest TJX Q2 capex refresh strengthens Q-05 with exact H1 spending of
`$1.159B`: Marmaxx `$690M`, HomeGoods `$146M`, TJX Canada `$190M`, and TJX
International `$133M`. These are segment allocations, not maintenance or
growth allocations; the replacement-versus-growth denominator remains open.
TJX's H1 operating cash flow of `$3.345B` less that capex produces a mechanical
`$2.186B` cash-after-property screen, which remains outside normalized owner
cash until maintenance, taxes, leases, and other burdens are allocated.

The latest retail upgrade adds a [post-financing residual screen](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-retail-post-financing-residual-screen-2026-09-15.md). It places disclosed H1 debt-principal repayment beside property spending, source-bounded temporary support, and stock-compensation sensitivity: TJX remains `$1.351B` after the support/SBC screen, Target reaches `-$473M`, and Walmart reaches `-$1.322B` under the stacked screen. These are not reported negative owner-cash results; they show how quickly a reported cash-after-property denominator can be exhausted when distinct senior claims are tested together. Leases remain inside operating cash flow and are not subtracted again, while maintenance capital, taxes, seasonality, attached-service economics, and entity-specific owner allocation remain open. The verifier now checks the three-row residual arithmetic and source route.

The retail common-period normalization surface now reconciles two distinct TJX
low-screen bundles: `$1.631B` after the `$470M` payable-support signal and
`$85M` stock compensation, versus `$1.351B` after the separate `$750M`
tariff/interchange support candidate and `$85M` stock compensation. These are
non-additive sensitivities; neither is promoted to normalized owner cash.

The Q-05 capex work now carries a comparable growth-floor control: Walmart's
H1 filing identifies `$1.087B` of new stores and clubs; TJX's forward FY2027
guide identifies `$222M` of new stores but cannot be substituted for H1; and
Target provides no H1 dollar floor despite naming remodels and new stores. The
Target blank is explicitly not zero growth spending, while Walmart's `$13.094B`
remainder is explicitly not maintenance. This sharpens the range without
promoting any figure to normalized owner cash.

The retail Q-04 quality-of-earnings layer now has an explicit same-period
double-count control. Balance-sheet inventory/payable signals are `-$116M`,
`-$261M`, and `-$1.492B` for TJX, Target, and Walmart, while the corresponding
cash-flow signals are `-$133M`, `-$333M`, and `-$1.012B`. The differences are
kept as timing and reconciliation fields; period-end supplier-finance balances
cannot be subtracted again without a settlement-date bridge. This improves the
cash-quality test without promoting balance movements to recurring owner cash.

The Walmart [H1 owner-cash denominator control](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-walmart-h1-owner-cash-denominator-control-2026-09-15.md)

`$3.226B` post-financing residual before repurchases, while explicitly holding

The [retail H1 owner-cash denominator handoff](combined-investment-research-retail-h1-owner-cash-denominator-handoff-2026-09-17.md)

control. It joins OCF/property spending to supplier-finance timing, support,

The [retail attached-services legal-owner and burden bridge](combined-investment-research-retail-attached-services-legal-owner-burden-bridge-2026-09-17.md)

The [Target H1 owner-cash denominator control](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-target-h1-owner-cash-denominator-control-2026-09-15.md)

The [retail cohort denominator control](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-retail-cohort-owner-cash-denominator-control-2026-09-15.md)

The [retail cohort owner-cash input schema](capital-flow-retail-owner-cash-input-schema-2026-09-16.md)

The Apollo earnings-to-owner bridge is now explicit and arithmetic-tested:

The Apollo fee-access lane now also has a [fee-rollforward boundary](capital-flow-apollo-athene-fee-rollforward-boundary-2026-09-15.md): Athene's opening management-fee payable, H1 expense, and ending payable imply a mechanically qualified `$779M` settlement amount. Athene's Q2 2026 filing strengthens that intermediate gate with a second dated

The latest connected-party upgrade adds Athene related-party investments of

The [Apollo–Athene credit-quality boundary](capital-flow-apollo-athene-credit-quality-return-boundary-2026-09-15.md) now adds related-party AFS exposure, aggregate mortgage delinquency, credit-risk loss, and impairment evidence. Apollo's FY2025 filing names `$466M` of contributions from ADIP and `$444M` of
distributions to ADIP in a context tagged to Athene and the Apollo Athene
Dedicated Investment Programs. This improves legal-entity flow traceability,
but does not turn the ADIP distribution into an AGM receipt or common-owner
cash.

The active lane is therefore:

```text
integrated research architecture
  -> three pilot chains
  -> two-sided upfront cash confirmation for Pilot 01
  -> incremental production-profile calibration
  -> delivery/settlement proof chase
  -> owner-cash and valuation model
  -> falsifier-led conclusion
```

current construction-cost estimate and the 1,170/100/95 MW ownership split;

owner cash. The evidence register now carries this as DAG-018,
and the expansion QoE overlay has twelve field-level diagnostics across the

change the active goal's 12-of-13 completion result or close CA-06.

## Interim end-to-end investment posture — 2026-09-17

The latest Q-03 official-source recheck preserves the funding-mix boundary:
the Q2 results packet confirms the April 1 `$4.300B` BHP payment, the
cash-on-hand/`$1.500B` term-loan/revolver source envelope, and H1 corporate
debt movement, but still provides no closing funds-flow, seller-account
confirmation, facility-specific draw allocation, Antamina repayment, or
asset-level tax and interest schedule. Q-03 therefore remains
`full-return-inputs-incomplete`; the next source request is now narrowly
defined rather than another company-level cash proxy.

The same live SEC-source pass also rechecked Q-07. Apollo and Athene still
expose source-side distributions and general HoldCo funding language, but no
AGM-only receiving-account, parent-only cash-flow, or controlled
intercompany-elimination record. Q-07 remains `searched-negative` for the
checked public perimeter, so the common-owner denominator cannot absorb the
Athene distribution or consolidated-cash figures by inference.

That pass also rechecked the retail denominator inputs. TJX, Target, and
Walmart's current official filings preserve the reported operating-cash and
property-spending bridges plus working-capital disclosures, but still do not
provide the maintenance/growth capex split, matched interim lease/tax cash, or
attached-service cost/collection allocation needed for normalized owner cash.
Q-04 through Q-06 therefore remain partial, with the next useful work defined
as a period-matched allocation or settlement record.

Q-08 also received a period-fresh liability-cost sensitivity: the current Q2
AMAPS 1 exposure of `$2.544B`, applied proportionally to the reconciled
`$158.852B` bond base and H1 `$5.749B` cost-of-funds pool, implies roughly
`$92.1M` of mechanical allocated cost. This improves denominator freshness but
is not observed asset-level funding cost, settlement, or return, so Q-08
remains `evidence-insufficient` for the decisive cash-and-claim join.

Q-10 also received a live official macro-input refresh: the September 16 FOMC
decision moved the federal-funds target to `3.75%–4.00%`; August CPI was `3.4%`
year over year with `2.4%` core and `16.3%` energy inflation; and July
disposable personal income rose `0.5%` month over month while PCE rose `0.2%`
and the saving rate was `3.0%`. These sharpen the rate, affordability, energy,
and liquidity stress markers but do not establish causal company attribution;
Q-10 remains `protocol-defined; descriptive diagnostic; evidence-insufficient`
for through-cycle promotion.

The integrated conclusion is a qualified research posture, not a buy/sell
recommendation:

| Pilot | What the evidence supports now | What prevents promotion | Decision-changing proof |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Wheaton–Antamina | A legally defined, long-life metal-credit stream with a confirmed `$4.300B` BHP receipt, first deliveries, and a visible financing envelope | BHP-only delivered credits, invoice/settlement cash, reserve-backed annual curve, tax and debt allocation | BHP-PMPA settlement statement or invoice joined to Wheaton receipt and a reserve-constrained delivery series |
| TJX / Target / Walmart | Affordability and substitution are plausible operating mechanisms with reported cash bridges, explicit support sensitivities, capex categories, and QoE diagnostics | Maintenance capital, recurring working capital, leases, taxes, attached-service cash, dilution, and comparable owner cash remain unresolved | Same-period recurring cash and claim schedules that close the five retail owner-cash promotion gates |
| Apollo–Athene | A real institutional-capital and retirement-services platform with statutory asset pools, named CUSIP routes, related-party flows, and parent-use surfaces | Athene-to-AGM receipt, elimination, borrower cash, liability-cost-adjusted asset return, and common-owner residual remain unproved | Bank/intercompany receipt ledger plus custodian/trustee, liability-cost, and parent-waterfall reconciliation |

The portfolio-level lesson is consistent across pilots: scale, accounting
income, reported operating cash, legal capacity, and a named transaction route
are useful evidence, but none is interchangeable with unrestricted common-owner
cash. The project should therefore preserve qualified valuation and expectation
screens, use financial-shenanigans and quality-of-earnings diagnostics as
falsifiers, and withhold final ranking until the named cash denominator and
claim waterfall are source-backed.

The 2026-09-17 narrowed local-source check applies the same move-on rule to
Q-11: no additional non-confidential Distribution Inspection billing,
collection, or funding schedule was found in the available FPL/PSC packet. The
lane is parked at `hold-with-strong-route-visible`; reopen it only when a
discovery response, supporting workpaper, or final SPPCRC true-up supplies a
joinable category allocation. This keeps the larger goal moving without
promoting aggregate utility recovery or cost into category owner cash.

Q-13 also advanced on 2026-09-17: URI's Q2 2026 10-Q reports `$2.802B` of ABL
borrowing capacity net of letters of credit and `$85M` of AR-securitization
capacity, a `$2.887B` facility-level total that reconciles to total liquidity
less cash. The end-to-end goal now has a cleaner legal-availability proxy, but
not a borrowing-base certificate, eligible-collateral/NOLV schedule, reserve
bridge, source-to-purchase ledger, or lifecycle return. The lane remains
open at its stated `evidence-insufficient` grade.
The live SEC recheck also located the June 18, 2026 AR-facility amendment;
it confirms the receivables-purchase legal route but adds no populated
purchaser availability schedule or certificate components. The next source
request remains a current certificate or lender/purchaser collateral report.

The [URI collateral-eligibility bridge](capital-flow-uri-abl-collateral-eligibility-bridge-2026-09-17.md)
now records the deeper contractual control: fleet equipment must pass eligibility,
lien, location, representation, and regulatory filters before NBV/NOLV and

Availability. This is a substantive cross-sector QoE insight, but it does not

The MF1 Q-12 lane now has a row-level statutory-to-servicing join boundary:

  Walmart FY2026 and Target FY2025 annual filings now add reconciled
  supplier-finance flows with `$40.062B` and `$12.066B` of confirmed invoices
  paid, respectively, but neither is a matched H1 allocation; the next
  required object remains settlement-date supplier-finance and working-capital
  reconciliation for the comparable period.
- **Annual denominator control:** the new [fiscal-year-aligned retail cohort
  denominator control](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-retail-annual-cohort-denominator-control-2026-09-17.md)
  puts TJX FY2026, Target FY2025, and Walmart FY2026 on one reported
  cash-after-property-per-diluted-share basis. Lease cash, tax cash, and the
  Target/Walmart annual supplier-finance movements are carried as separate
  context and never subtracted twice. This strengthens comparability but leaves
  all three rows reported-not-normalized because maintenance capital, attached
  services, seasonal timing, and claim allocation remain open.
- **Q-05 reinvestment:** TJX, Target, and Walmart have category and timing
  evidence, but no company-disclosed maintenance/replacement split; the next
  required object is a period-matched maintenance-capital schedule or bounded
  project ledger.
- **Q-06 attached services:** Target advertising/card/other revenue and
  Walmart membership-and-other income are visible, but service-level cost,
  collection, capital, tax, and legal-entity cash are not joined; the lane
  remains `evidence-insufficient`. Target's FY2025 filing now adds a legal and
  accounting perimeter for Roundel, TD-owned card receivables, Target Plus,
  Shipt, and membership revenue, but does not allocate the service burden to
  cash. Walmart's FY2026 filing now adds segment membership-and-other income
  and a `$4.4B` membership-fee denominator, but its service costs remain
  unallocated.
- **Annual attached-services frontier:** the new [annual attached-services cash
  frontier](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-retail-annual-attached-services-cash-frontier-2026-09-17.md)
  aligns Target FY2025's `$2.063B` and Walmart FY2026's `$6.750B` reported
  attached-service pools with the annual denominator screen through a
  hypothetical 0–100% conversion range. It remains outside owner cash until
  service collection and allocated burden fields are joined.
- **Annual reported-cash expectation screen:** the [dated annual market-burden
  screen](combined-investment-research-pilot-02-retail-annual-reported-cash-expectation-screen-2026-09-17.md)
  divides September 17 market caps by reported annual cash-after-property for
  TJX (`27.906x`), Target (`24.913x`), and Walmart (`57.471x`). These are
  expectation screens, not normalized owner-cash multiples or intrinsic values.

partial; no retail proxy is promoted to normalized owner cash.

## Latest integration milestone — 2026-09-17

The [annual-report cross-sector integration matrix](annual-report-cross-sector-integration-matrix-pass-1.md)
now folds the eight article-theme expansions—services, consumer goods, basic
materials, real estate, broad technology, healthcare care infrastructure,
energy, and ordinary finance—back into the four established pillars of
affordability, contractual control, institutional capital, and permissioned
power capital. Each row explicitly names the force, control point, payer,
burden carrier, sector-specific denominator, QoE/financial-shenanigans
reconciliation prompt, owner-cash/valuation object, macro-liquidity route, and
decisive breaker. Its [structured companion](data/annual-report-cross-sector-integration-matrix-pass-1.csv)
is the machine-readable handoff.

This closes the current breadth-to-integration step. It does not close the
larger goal: the matrix still routes the next work to named receipts,
same-period cash allocation, populated collateral certificates, project
returns, liability-adjusted spreads, and common-owner residuals rather than
promoting narrative breadth into a cross-sector ranking.

The next move-on sector is now the [restaurant-franchise first-principles
synthesis](annual-report-restaurant-franchise-first-principles-synthesis-pass-1-2026-09-17.md).
It applies the full chain to CAVA, Restaurant Brands International, Wingstop,
and Yum: consumer affordability and brand control, franchisee versus
franchisor burden, royalty and advertising-fund perimeter, FY2025 OCF/capex,
financial-shenanigans diagnostics, liquidity stress, and diluted owner-cash
promotion gates. The `$26.141M–$1.639B` mechanical OCF-less-PP&E surfaces are
explicitly diagnostic and non-comparable; no restaurant ranking or normalized
owner-cash conclusion is promoted.
The [restaurant-franchise owner-cash promotion workbench](combined-investment-research-restaurant-franchise-owner-cash-promotion-workbench-2026-09-17.md)
now turns that lane into an auditable promotion matrix: systemwide sales,
royalties, advertising funds, franchisee health, support, capex, debt, leases,
acquisitions, and diluted common-owner cash are kept as separate fields. The
restaurant cohort remains qualified and unranked until a same-period
royalty/collection and franchisee-health schedule is available.
The [restaurant current-period refresh](combined-investment-research-restaurant-current-period-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
adds McDonald's and Chipotle H1 2026 evidence with a strict period-control
verifier. It keeps their company-specific OCF, PP&E, distributions, unit

The [insurance broker/carrier synthesis](annual-report-insurance-broker-carrier-first-principles-synthesis-pass-1-2026-09-17.md)

The [exchange/information infrastructure synthesis](annual-report-exchange-information-infrastructure-first-principles-synthesis-pass-1-2026-09-17.md)

The [industrial distribution and building-materials synthesis](annual-report-industrial-distribution-building-materials-first-principles-synthesis-pass-1-2026-09-17.md)
