and tax lines. It confirms a searched-negative result while preserving visible
lease liabilities, noncash additions, tax provisions, and accrual movements;
it does not assume zero payments or close CA-06. This is D-59.

The [retail Q2 2026 working-capital denominator refresh](combined-investment-research-retail-q2-2026-working-capital-denominator-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
adds current same-period OCF, property, inventory, payable, lease, tax, and
dilution controls for TJX, Target, and Walmart. It improves CA-06's QoE and
timing surface but does not promote OCF-less-property or management-defined FCF
to normalized common-owner cash. This is D-60.

The [retail Q2 2026 capex allocation sensitivity](combined-investment-research-retail-q2-2026-capex-allocation-sensitivity-2026-09-17.md)
advances Q-05 by isolating Walmart's disclosed `$1.087B` growth category while
keeping the remaining property pool mixed. Target and TJX still lack a
same-period maintenance/growth dollar split, so no normalized owner-cash
promotion is made. This is D-61.

The [retail Q2 2026 inventory/payables normalization](combined-investment-research-retail-q2-2026-inventory-payables-normalization-2026-09-17.md)
advances Q-04 by reconciling ending inventory/payables balances to the reported
cash-flow changes for TJX, Target, and Walmart. It explicitly prohibits
double-counting those operating movements against OCF and keeps supplier
finance, reversal, margin, and normalized owner-cash questions open. This is
D-62.

The [retail Q2 2026 supplier-finance obligation refresh](combined-investment-research-retail-q2-2026-supplier-finance-obligation-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
advances Q-04 by documenting the current obligation perimeter: Target's
supplier-finance-eligible obligations are `$3.2B` versus `$3.0B` at January 31,
2026, and Walmart's outstanding supplier-finance obligations are `$6.4B`
versus `$6.0B`. The filings distinguish eligible or outstanding obligations
from actual early payments and settlement cash, so the refresh does not
subtract these balances from OCF. Supplier-finance settlement remains
unproven, TJX has no comparable note in the reviewed interim packet, and
CA-06 remains partial. This is D-63.

The [broad technology Q2 2026 cash-quality refresh](combined-investment-research-broad-technology-q2-2026-cash-quality-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
advances the next move-on sector with period-controlled Fortinet and Cloudflare
evidence. Fortinet's H1 OCF and reported FCF are `$2.121B` and `$1.972B`, but
deferred-revenue growth, SBC, repurchases, and debt repayment remain visible
claims. Cloudflare's H1 revenue grew 35%, while gross margin fell to 72% from
75%, H1 SBC was `$274.1M`, and PP&E plus capitalized software was `$135.4M`.
Billings, RPO, reported FCF, and noncash SBC add-backs remain diagnostic rather
than normalized owner cash. The broad-technology lane is qualified and
unranked. This is D-64.

The [basic materials Q2 2026 cash-quality refresh](combined-investment-research-basic-materials-q2-2026-cash-quality-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
advances the input-scarcity lane with current CF Industries, Sherwin-Williams,
and West Fraser evidence. It separates CF's `$220M` of litigation and
insurance proceeds, `$194M` of tax deposits, and partner-funded Blue Point
capital from recurring nitrogen cash; Sherwin-Williams' `$1.487B` operating
cash from working-capital, lease, acquisition, SBC, and treasury-stock claims;
and West Fraser's Q2 recovery cash from its H1 capex and cycle burden. The
sector remains cycle-qualified, owner-cash-open, and unranked. This is D-65.

The [consumer goods and household upkeep Q2 2026 cash-quality refresh](combined-investment-research-consumer-goods-q2-2026-cash-quality-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
advances the household-value lane with Burlington, Ollie's, and Lowe's current
filings. It separates Burlington's `$55.5M` tariff-refund benefit and `$537.5M`
of property/lease-acquisition spending from operating cash; Ollie's inventory
and new-store funding from its `$153.6M` H1 OCF; and Lowe's `$7.009B` OCF from
capex, dividends, repurchases, and bond maturities. The cohort remains
household-value-qualified, owner-cash-open, and unranked. This is D-66.

The [ordinary finance Q2 2026 credit-quality refresh](combined-investment-research-ordinary-finance-q2-2026-credit-quality-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
advances the bank-card-and-payment lane with current JPMorgan, American
Express, and Capital One evidence. It separates JPMorgan's `$4.55B` Visa-share
gain from recurring earnings; American Express's reserve-release and
write-off relationship from billed business; and Capital One's `$3.0B` net
income from `$3.642B` of net charge-offs, a `$662M` allowance release, and
Discover integration adjustments. The cohort remains credit-quality-qualified,
loss-normalization-and-owner-cash-open, and unranked. This is D-67.

The [energy affordability and supply-route Q2 2026 cash-quality refresh](combined-investment-research-energy-q2-2026-cash-quality-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
advances the molecule-logistics and conversion lane with current Energy
Transfer, Cheniere, PBF, and Devon evidence. It separates capacity, cargoes,
and adjusted DCF from project cash; PBF's `$1.2651B` H1 OCF from `$242.4M` of
turnaround spending; and Devon's `$3.7B` Q2 OCF from `$1.269B` capex and the
`$2.6B` Delaware acreage purchase. The lane remains physical-route-qualified,
cycle-and-project-return-open, and unranked. This is D-68.

The [integrated oil and gas Q2 2026 cash-quality refresh](combined-investment-research-integrated-oil-gas-q2-2026-cash-quality-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
extends the energy lane into ConocoPhillips and Exxon Mobil. It separates H1
operating cash from production decline, reserve replacement, development and
refining capital, working-capital timing, derivative and reserve items,
geopolitical disruption, contingent consideration, debt, and shareholder
returns. The lane remains physical-asset-qualified,
cycle-and-replacement-open, and unranked. This is D-69.

The [Q2 2026 cross-sector cash-quality control panel](combined-investment-research-q2-2026-cross-sector-cash-quality-control-panel-2026-09-17.md)
connects the latest broad-technology, basic-materials, consumer-goods,
ordinary-finance, energy-supply-route, and integrated-oil-and-gas refreshes on
one period-aware surface. It preserves the distinction between deferred
service revenue, exceptional proceeds, inventory-funded growth, credit-loss
normalization, project cash, and reserve replacement, while refusing to pool
unlike denominators into a ranking. This is D-70.

The [Q2 2026 valuation and liquidity stress matrix](combined-investment-research-q2-2026-valuation-liquidity-stress-matrix-2026-09-17.md)
extends those six lanes through explicit Damodaran-style valuation objects,
reinvestment variables, Lyn Alden-style liquidity stresses, and filing-based
thesis breakers. It keeps price-implied expectations separate from proven cash
and preserves the no-ranking boundary. This is D-71.

The [Wheaton–Antamina Q-03 metal-credit receipt boundary](capital-flow-wheaton-antamina-q03-metal-credit-receipt-boundary-2026-09-17.md)
now closes an integration gap in the original capital-flow pilot. It identifies
the legally correct receipt object—BHP metal-credit issuance, sale or
receivable, and Wheaton bank collection—rather than searching for physical
silver delivery. BHP's `$4.3B` upfront receipt and Wheaton's combined Q2
production/timing observations remain evidenced, while BHP-only credits,
invoice price, collection, tax, and financing allocation remain open. This is
D-72.

The [Q-10 current six-lane macro overlay](combined-investment-research-q10-current-six-lane-macro-overlay-2026-09-17.md)
extends the through-cycle causal protocol to the latest technology, materials,
consumer, finance, energy-route, and integrated-oil-and-gas lanes. It records
the predicted macro transmission, company observable, confound, and required
repeated evidence while keeping causal promotion open. This is D-73.

The [Q2 2026 QoE and financial-shenanigans diagnostic panel](combined-investment-research-q2-2026-qoe-financial-shenanigans-panel-2026-09-17.md)
now makes the forensic overlay explicit for those six lanes. It maps deferred
revenue, exceptional proceeds, inventory and working-capital timing, reserve
releases, adjusted metrics, derivative effects, acquisition claims, and
replacement burdens to the exact reconciliation required. It preserves the
Sloan/Schilit prompt framework, rejects a non-comparable pooled Beneish score,
and makes no fraud finding or owner-cash promotion. This is D-74.

The [undercovered-industry theme synthesis](annual-report-undercovered-industry-theme-synthesis-pass-1.md)
now becomes a first-class end-to-end deliverable. Its eight rows turn the
519-company breadth into article-ready themes across services, consumer goods,
materials, real estate, technology, healthcare, energy, and ordinary finance,
while preserving the distinction between social/operating insight and named-
cash proof. This is D-75.

The [Q-03 receivable-classification boundary](capital-flow-wheaton-antamina-q03-receivable-classification-boundary-2026-09-17.md)
adds a narrower public-source test to the metal-credit route. Wheaton's Q2
receivable categories and credit-sale policy show what is and is not identified
in the filing, strengthening the searched-negative classification boundary
without treating an unallocated category as BHP cash. This is D-76.

The [URI ABL collateral-eligibility bridge](capital-flow-uri-abl-collateral-eligibility-bridge-2026-09-17.md)
now makes Q-13's contractual availability measurement explicit. It separates
eligible rental equipment, reserves, appraisals, U.S./Canadian borrowing bases,
and Combined Availability from URI's reported liquidity and debt, preserving
the missing populated-certificate and lifecycle-return gate. This is D-77.

The [PBF redemption settlement bridge](capital-flow-pbf-redemption-settlement-bridge-pass-1.md)
now records a completed source/use event: `$492.1M` of net 2034-note proceeds
plus available cash funded redemption of `$801.6M` of 2028 notes on June 25,
2026. It keeps trustee settlement, accrued interest, cash-on-hand allocation,
and refinancing NPV as explicit open gates. This is D-78.

The [Ares/Frontline primary-source refresh](capital-flow-ares-frontline-primary-source-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
dates the private-credit route across Ares's 2025 acquisition and 2026 growth
disclosures and adds the SEC-filed Frontline instrument marker. It improves
role, date, and instrument control without converting arranger status, fair
value, or commitments into funded lender cash. This is D-79.

The [PBF September conditional-redemption boundary](capital-flow-pbf-september-2026-conditional-redemption-liquidity-boundary-2026-09-17.md)
adds a current debt-stack event: an issued `$550M` 2032 exchangeable-note
financing with approximately `$533.6M` of net proceeds, alongside the still-
conditional redemption of `$500M` of 2030 notes at `103.938%` plus accrued
interest. It is kept separate from the completed June redemption and does not
promote issuance proceeds or an unsettled redemption to NPV or owner return.
This is D-80.

The [insurance broker/carrier Q2 cash-quality refresh](combined-investment-research-insurance-brokers-carrier-q2-2026-cash-quality-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
extends current-period QoE coverage to Marsh, Arthur J. Gallagher, and Chubb.
It separates fiduciary cash and broker acquisition/earnout burdens from carrier
premiums, unpaid losses, policy benefits, and adjusted operating cash. The
models remain incomparable for ranking and statutory/common-owner return remains
open. This is D-81.

The [exchange and information infrastructure Q2 refresh](combined-investment-research-exchange-information-infrastructure-q2-2026-cash-quality-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
adds CME and S&P Global to the current-period control-point layer. It separates
clearing collateral and guaranty-fund flows from corporate cash, and separates
S&P subscription/transaction economics from disposition proceeds, acquisitions,
amortization, SBC, and non-GAAP free cash flow. This is D-82.

The [materials, specialty-chemicals, and steel Q2 refresh](combined-investment-research-materials-chemicals-steel-q2-2026-cash-quality-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
adds Ecolab's embedded chemistry/service model and Nucor's flexible steel
capacity model. It makes working-capital investment, acquisition/SBC effects,
project capital, and cycle dependence explicit rather than treating improved
operating cash as through-cycle owner cash. This is D-83.

The [digital real-estate Q2 cash-quality refresh](combined-investment-research-digital-real-estate-q2-2026-cash-quality-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
joins Equinix and Digital Realty in one current-period comparison. It separates
development and recurring capex, acquisitions, AFFO/FFO, partner and equity
funding, debt, commitments, backlog, and dilution, preserving the project-return
and common-owner-cash gates. This is D-84.

The [healthcare-distribution current-period synthesis](combined-investment-research-healthcare-distribution-current-period-synthesis-2026-09-17.md)
now makes McKesson, Cencora, and Cardinal's different fiscal periods and cash
conversion problems explicit. It separates distributor working capital,
acquisition/legal claims, preferred/NCI interests, and dilution from any
normalized common-owner conclusion. This is D-85.

The [promotion-gate execution ledger](combined-investment-research-promotion-gate-execution-ledger-2026-09-17.md)
converts the remaining open work into 13 named source objects, promotion
tests, and stop rules across Q-03, CA-06, URI, PBF, private credit, physical
capacity, healthcare, devices, KLA, power/insurance, Atwell, restaurants,
and integrated oil and gas. It is D-86 and makes the next execution order
auditable without turning qualified diagnostics into unsupported rankings.

The [TJX Q2 FY2027 settlement and capex boundary](combined-investment-research-retail-tjx-q2-2026-settlement-capex-boundary-2026-09-17.md)
adds a new primary-filing QoE control: H1 operating cash was affected by a
credit-card interchange-fee settlement and tariff refunds, while capex mixed
renovations, new stores, distribution centers, offices, and IT. The amount,
maintenance split, service costs, and owner-cash residual remain open; the
filing does disclose `$1.147B` of H1 operating lease cash paid. This is D-87.

The [URI Q2 2026 availability and lifecycle boundary](combined-investment-research-uri-q2-2026-availability-lifecycle-boundary-2026-09-17.md)
adds a same-period asset-backed bridge: `$2.999B` stated liquidity, `$1.779B`
receivables collateral, `$3.305B` OCF, `$2.885B` purchases, `$706M` sale
proceeds, and `$1.149B` company-defined FCF. Equipment borrowing-base/NOLV,
cohort allocation, and lifecycle return remain open. This is D-88.

The [Target Q2 2026 cash-quality boundary](combined-investment-research-retail-target-q2-2026-cash-quality-boundary-2026-09-17.md)
adds the same-period Target bridge: `$4.519B` H1 OCF, `$2.404B` property
spending, `$994M` tariff refunds, and `$3.2B` eligible supplier-finance
obligations that are not actual early payments. Target lease cash,
maintenance/service allocation, and common-owner residual remain open. This is
D-89.

The [Walmart Q2 FY2027 capex and tariff-refund boundary](combined-investment-research-retail-walmart-q2-fy27-capex-refund-boundary-2026-09-17.md)
adds the current Walmart bridge: `$2.9B` tariff refunds, `$19.710B` H1 OCF,
`$14.181B` capex, and `$5.529B` management-defined FCF, with disclosed
supply-chain, remodel, and new-store categories. Walmart's FCF excludes debt
service and acquisitions; maintenance, lease, service, and common-owner
allocation remain open. This is D-90.

The [Wheaton Investor Day forward-profile boundary](capital-flow-wheaton-antamina-investor-day-forward-profile-boundary-2026-09-16.md)
is now integrated into the Q-02/Q-03 route: management forecasts approximately
`6.0 Moz` of Antamina silver annually for the first five years and `5.4 Moz`
for the first ten years, but the presentation does not prove reserve-backed
delivery, BHP-only credit settlement, invoice cash, tax allocation, or
financed return. This preserves the expectation-versus-proof gate.

The [Q-03 metal-credit boundary](capital-flow-wheaton-antamina-q03-metal-credit-receipt-boundary-2026-09-17.md)
now adds a contract-economics calculation without confusing it with realized
cash. BHP's `33.75%` stream multiplied by the fixed `90%` payable factor implies
a `30.375%` contractual payable entitlement before the `100M`-ounce threshold;
the post-threshold `22.5%` stream implies `20.25%`. These are scenario inputs,
not BHP-only delivered ounces, credit sales, or bank receipts. The combined
`67.5%` Antamina figure remains an aggregation of BHP and legacy Glencore
streams, so Q-03's receipt, tax, financing-allocation, and after-tax return
gates remain open. This is D-93.

The Q-03 route also adds Wheaton's current company-level burden context: Q2
operating cash flow of `$650M`, approximately `$1.4B` for H1, and management's
statement that higher interest expense partly reflected debt drawn to fund the
BHP PMPA. This improves financing stress visibility but cannot be allocated to
the BHP stream or netted into its return without a facility, tax, and interest
schedule. This is D-96.

The [URI Q-13 availability boundary](combined-investment-research-uri-q2-2026-availability-lifecycle-boundary-2026-09-17.md)
adds a legal-entity cash constraint: United Rentals, Inc. is principally a
holding company, while operations run primarily through URNA and its
subsidiaries, whose credit agreements restrict transfers to the shareholder.
The `$112M` parent cash, URNA cash, facility capacity, and `$3.305B` consolidated
OCF therefore cannot be treated as one freely distributable common-owner pool.
Permitted distributions, debt-service/covenant limits, intercompany transfers,
and parent receipts remain required before FCF can be promoted to owner cash.
The populated equipment borrowing-base and fleet-cohort lifecycle-return gates
also remain open. This is D-98.

The [PBF September conditional-redemption boundary](capital-flow-pbf-september-2026-conditional-redemption-liquidity-boundary-2026-09-17.md)
now adds a calculated funding floor: redeeming `$500M` of 2030 notes at
`103.938%` implies `$519.690M` of principal plus redemption premium before
accrued interest. The amount is a scenario calculation, not settled cash; the
offering remains conditional, and fees, taxes, accrued interest, post-close
liquidity, dilution, and refinancing NPV remain open. This is D-95.

The [restaurant current-period refresh](combined-investment-research-restaurant-current-period-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
adds a sharper franchisee-health and revenue-perimeter control. McDonald's
reported `44,016` franchised restaurants out of `46,028` total and Q2
franchised sales of `$34.451B`, while explicitly stating that franchised sales
are not company revenue. The sales base supports rent, royalty, and fee revenue,
but it is not collected franchisor cash; franchisee funding of remodels,
technology, labor, and required reinvestment remains a separate burden. The
same-period franchisee collection, closure, delinquency, and reinvestment join
is not public in the reviewed filing, so the restaurant lane remains qualified,
owner-cash-open, and unranked. This is D-91.

The [insurance broker/carrier refresh](combined-investment-research-insurance-brokers-carrier-q2-2026-cash-quality-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
now adds a quantified Chubb liability and catastrophe boundary. H1 net unpaid
losses ended at `$71.216B`, gross unpaid losses at `$89.669B`, reinsurance
recoverable at `$18.453B`, and net losses paid at `$11.353B`; Chubb also reported
`$584M` of favorable prior-year reserve development. Its modeled 1-in-100
pre-tax catastrophe PML was `$5.650B` worldwide and `$3.766B` for U.S. hurricane
risk, the latter equal to 5.0% of shareholders' equity under the model's
assumptions. These figures improve the reserve, reinsurance, and liquidity
stress test but do not prove reserve adequacy, statutory availability, or
common-owner cash. This is D-92.

The [valuation and liquidity stress matrix](combined-investment-research-q2-2026-valuation-liquidity-stress-matrix-2026-09-17.md)
now carries the latest named-object inputs into the Damodaran/Lyn Alden layer:
Wheaton entitlement and debt burden, URI parent-availability restrictions,
PBF's conditional redemption floor, and Chubb's reserve/reinsurance and
catastrophe stress. These are expectation and funding inputs, not promoted
owner cash or returns; the matrix remains no-ranking. This is D-97.

The [current end-to-end synthesis](combined-investment-research-current-synthesis.md)
now exposes the valuation/liquidity overlay alongside the newest sector
boundaries. This closes the reader-facing handoff from filing fact to
Damodaran expectation burden and Lyn Alden-style funding stress while keeping
the underlying claims qualified and unranked. This is D-98.

The same synthesis now surfaces the healthcare-distribution period and
legal-claim boundary in the latest execution section: McKesson, Cencora, and
Cardinal remain non-comparable by fiscal period, and Cardinal's `$374M` July
opioid payment is kept outside FY2026 cash. This preserves period matching and
prevents acquisition, legal, preferred/NCI, or dilution claims from being
hidden inside a cross-company OCF comparison. This is D-99.

The latest execution section also surfaces medical devices as a distinct
clinical-workflow lane: Stryker's Inari acquisition/claim burden, Intuitive's
installed-base utilization and lease-collection gate, and Henry Schein's
securitization, restructuring, debt, and dilution burden remain company-
specific. This extends the end-to-end method without pooling unlike OCF
denominators or promoting procedure growth to owner cash. This is D-100.

The latest execution section now also includes exchange and information
infrastructure: CME collateral and guaranty-fund movements are not treated as
corporate cash, and S&P Global disposition proceeds, acquisitions, purchased-
intangible amortization, and SBC are not treated as recurring information
owner cash. This extends the control-point map from physical and clinical
infrastructure to financial-market liquidity and institutional information
workflows. This is D-101.

The latest execution section now carries the [materials, specialty chemicals,
and steel Q2 refresh](combined-investment-research-materials-chemicals-steel-q2-2026-cash-quality-refresh-2026-09-17.md)
into the reader-facing synthesis. Ecolab's H1 operating cash of `$1.1754B`
against `$588.6M` of capex included `$197.0M` of receivables use, `$131.4M` of
inventory use, `$75.1M` of SBC, and `$60M` of Ovivo-related equity-incentive
payments. Nucor's `$2.286B` of operating cash against `$1.232B` of capex
included `$952M` of receivables use and `$560M` of inventory use, with 2026
capex estimated at `$2.50B`. This keeps embedded chemistry/service and flexible
steel capacity visible as distinct control points while leaving organic owner
cash, maintenance-versus-growth capital, and through-cycle returns open. This
is D-102.

The Q-03 route now adds a period-matched combined Antamina segment surface from
Wheaton's Q2 financial statements: Q2 sales were `$150.549M` and operating cash
flow `$122.039M`; H1 sales were `$277.563M` and operating cash flow `$222.223M`.
The figures strengthen the same-entity operating and cash denominator, but they
cover the BHP and legacy Glencore streams together and cannot be assigned to
the BHP PMPA. The BHP-specific metal-credit issue, sale/receivable, bank
collection, tax, and facility-allocation objects remain open. This is D-103.

The CA-06 retail route now has a stronger TJX settlement object from the Q2
FY2027 Form 10-Q: TJX reported a non-recurring `$419M` credit-card interchange
settlement gain net of legal expenses, and stated that related amounts were
received during the quarter ended May 2, 2026 and included in H1 operating
activities. This closes the previously missing receipt period and quantifies a
net accounting benefit, while preserving the gross cash/legal-expense, tax,
maintenance-capex, and common-owner allocation gates. This is D-104.

The Q-13 URI route also received a targeted public-filing negative boundary.
The June 30, 2026 10-Q reports covenant availability and facility components,
but the checked filing contains no populated NOLV or equipment borrowing-base
schedule. The absence is recorded only as a searched-negative for the public
filing; it is not treated as proof that a private lender certificate does not
exist. The equipment-collateral, source-to-purchase, and fleet-cohort return
gates therefore remain open. This is D-105.

The private-credit lane now has a reader-facing Q2 source boundary for Bear
Financing. The official Accordia Q2 2026 verification document reports an
aggregate `$7.371492B` bond base but omits the detailed `90231*-AA-0` Bear
Financing Schedule D row. This narrows the next source request to the full
Schedule D or a custodian/loan ledger, while preserving the rule that a missing
accessible row cannot be treated as evidence of sale, repayment, or settlement.
Lender allocation, borrower use, debt service, receipt, and liability-adjusted
return remain open. This is D-106.

The industrial-conversion lane is now surfaced in the reader-facing execution
handoff. Sterling's H1 operating cash of `$328.021M` is shown after visible
capex, cash acquisitions, and earn-out uses, leaving `$110.623M` before the
remaining owner claims. WESCO's Q2 cash conversion weakened despite strong
demand, and Fastenal's OCF conversion fell to 69.4% of net income. The lane
therefore carries project-accounting, acquisition-cohort, working-capital, and
distribution-capital warnings without pooling unlike denominators or promoting
reported backlog and distributions to owner cash. This is D-107.
The power-grid current-period synthesis is now surfaced in the central
execution handoff. FPL's H1 OCF/capex surface was `$5.388B` versus `$5.780B`,
AEP's was `$3.421B` versus `$5.606B` of construction spending, and Duke's was
`$4.272B` versus `$8.240B` of capex. The three demand/recovery objects remain
distinct—FPL category recovery, AEP signed large-load expectations, and Duke's
shared Anderson County project—and none is treated as billed, collected, or
common-owner cash without the named legal-entity and customer-recovery join.
This is D-108.

The integrated oil-and-gas refresh is now surfaced in the central execution
handoff. ConocoPhillips' H1 OCF of `$11.729B` against `$5.972B` of capital and
investments coincided with production declining to `2.278M BOE/d`; Exxon’s
`$32.260B` OCF against `$12.997B` of PP&E additions included a `$3.857B`
operational working-capital use and identified losses. These inputs sharpen
the price, decline, replacement, claims, and margin stress tests without
promoting mechanical OCF-less-capex or capital returns to normalized owner
cash. This is D-109.

The consumer-goods and household-upkeep refresh is now surfaced in the central
execution handoff. Burlington's `$334.6M` H1 OCF included a `$55.5M`
tariff-refund benefit while `$532.4M` went to property spending; Ollie's
`$153.6M` OCF remained exposed to inventory and store-growth timing; and
Lowe's `$7.009B` OCF coexisted with capex, dividends, repurchases, and roughly
`$2.4B` of bond maturities. This extends the financial-shenanigans/QoE screen
to inventory liquidity, store payback, service capital, and refinancing without
ranking unlike retail models. This is D-110.

The ordinary-finance Q2 refresh is now surfaced in the central execution
handoff. JPMorgan's `$4.55B` Visa-share gain is separated from its `$2.5B`
credit costs and `$2.4B` net charge-offs; American Express's `$455.8B` billed
business is separated from credit-loss provision and reserve-release effects;
and Capital One's `$3.642B` net charge-offs are kept alongside its `$662M`
allowance release and integration costs. This extends the QoE and
financial-shenanigans controls to reserve normalization, funding, capital, and
dilution without ranking unlike financial platforms. This is D-111.

The broad-technology refresh is now surfaced in the central execution handoff.
Fortinet's `$2.121B` H1 OCF includes a `$7.676B` deferred-revenue base, SBC,
repurchases, and debt repayment; Cloudflare's `$275.9M` OCF and `$140.5M` FCF
remain alongside SBC, PP&E, capitalized software, and acquisition spending.
This keeps service renewal, retention, infrastructure replacement, contract
acquisition, dilution, and cost-to-serve in the owner-cash test without treating
billings, RPO, or reported FCF as recurring residual cash. This is D-112.

The current healthcare-distribution refresh is now surfaced in the central
execution handoff. McKesson's Q1 FY2027 used `$220M` of operating cash while
receivables and inventory used `$4.448B` against `$3.773B` of payable support;
the `$1.25B` Apollo preferred investment and `$2.6B` of shareholder returns
remain separate legal-entity and funding claims. Cencora's 9M FY2026 `$1.688B`
OCF coexisted with `$511M` capex and `$4.974B` acquisition cash, while
Cardinal's FY2026 `$5.174B` OCF coexisted with `$649M` capex, `$1.991B`
acquisitions, opioid payments, debt, and distributions. This preserves the
QoE/financial-shenanigans controls for working-capital timing, acquisition
returns, legal cash, preferred claims, and dilution without pooling unlike
periods or promoting reported OCF to common-owner cash. This is D-113.

The healthcare-access and care-delivery cluster is now surfaced separately from
healthcare distribution. UnitedHealth and Cigna cover benefits, pharmacy, and
administrative control; DaVita covers recurring dialysis; Option Care covers
alternate-site infusion; and Addus, BrightSpring, and Enhabit cover home and
community labor-backed delivery. UnitedHealth's Q2 revenue was `$112.0B` with
2026 operating-cash guidance near `$24.0B`, while Cigna's Q2 revenue was
`$71.668B` with `$2.054B` adjusted income from operations. The QoE gate remains
medical-cost trend, claims/reserve conversion, reimbursement, labor, quality,
trust, regulation, and legal-entity cash—not revenue scale or adjusted income.
This is D-120.

The digital-infrastructure refresh is now surfaced in the central execution
handoff. Equinix's H1 `$1.784B` OCF was below `$2.834B` of PP&E purchases
before `$224M` of real-estate acquisitions; Digital Realty's `$1.595B` OCF
was roughly `$32M` below `$1.627B` of cash-basis capex, including `$1.477B`
development and `$136M` recurring capex, alongside approximately `$2.5B` of
ATM proceeds and `$18.768B` debt. This extends the QoE and financial-
shenanigans screen to development-capital opacity, private/fund capital,
leasing and rent commencement, debt, partner claims, and dilution without
treating AFFO/FFO, backlog, signed rent, or equity proceeds as normalized
owner cash. This is D-114.

The insurance broker/carrier refresh is now surfaced in the central execution
handoff. Chubb's Q2 operating cash was `$3.73B`, but H1 net unpaid losses were
`$71.216B`, gross unpaid losses `$89.669B`, reinsurance recoverable `$18.453B`,
and net losses paid `$11.353B`; `$584M` of favorable prior-year reserve
development remains a normalization input, not recurring underwriting cash.
This extends the QoE and financial-shenanigans screen to reserve development,
claims paid, reinsurance collectibility, catastrophe exposure, statutory
capital, and policyholder liabilities without ranking broker and carrier
models or promoting operating cash to common-owner residual. This is D-115.

The restaurant-franchise current-period refresh is now surfaced in the central
execution handoff. McDonald's reported `$34.451B` of Q2 franchised sales across
`44,016` franchised restaurants, but the filing states that franchised sales
are not company revenue and only indicate franchisee health. McDonald's H1
`$5.222B` OCF, `$1.516B` PP&E spending, `$1.251B` repurchases, and `$2.640B`
dividends, plus Chipotle's `$1.332B` OCF and `$397.601M` PP&E, remain
period-specific diagnostics. Royalty/fee collection, franchisee cash health,
closures, delinquency, remodel/technology reinvestment, and diluted owner cash
remain unjoined. This is D-117.

The exchange-and-information-infrastructure refresh is now surfaced in the
central execution handoff. CME's H1 operating cash was `$2.207B`, while its
`$138.5B` Federal Reserve cash account and `$1.545B` decrease in
performance-bond/guaranty-fund contributions remain collateral and clearing-
liquidity surfaces rather than unrestricted corporate cash. S&P Global's
`$2.476B` operating cash and `$2.249B` reported FCF sat alongside `$361M`
disposition proceeds, `$26M` acquisitions, `$551M` intangible amortization,
and `$95M` SBC. This keeps volume, collateral, acquisition, intangible,
repurchase, and dilution claims separate from organic common-owner cash
without ranking unlike financial-infrastructure models. This is D-116.

The QoE/financial-shenanigans diagnostic panel is now expanded beyond the
original six lanes. It adds healthcare distribution, digital infrastructure,
insurance/statutory cash, exchange/information infrastructure, and restaurant
franchising, mapping each sector's signal to a same-entity, same-period
reconciliation: working-capital and acquisition cash, project and replacement
capital, reserves and reinsurance, collateral and acquired cohorts, or
franchisee collections and reinvestment. The panel preserves Sloan/Schilit
diagnostic prompts, rejects a pooled Beneish score across incomparable
perimeters, and makes no fraud finding or owner-cash promotion. This is D-118.

The cross-sector cash-quality control panel now carries the expanded current
lanes rather than stopping at technology, materials, consumer, finance, and
energy. Healthcare, digital infrastructure, insurance/statutory, exchange/
information, and restaurant franchising now each have a period-labeled cash
surface, distortion, correct owner-cash question, and promotion status. The
panel preserves the central rule—same period, same legal entity, named cash or
claim object, and reconciliation path—and does not create a pooled ranking.
This is D-119.

The valuation/liquidity matrix now carries the care-delivery and expanded
move-on lanes through explicit Damodaran-style valuation objects, reinvestment
variables, Lyn Alden-style funding stresses, and filing-based breakers. The
healthcare rows separate claims and reimbursement from treatment/service labor;
the digital, insurance, exchange, and restaurant rows separately model project,
reserve, collateral, acquired-cohort, franchisee, and diluted-owner claims.
Price-implied expectations remain distinct from proven cash and no ranking is
created. This is D-121.

The healthcare-access current-period cash refresh now adds primary filing
controls: UnitedHealth's Q2 `$11.1B` operating cash, `86.7%` medical-cost ratio,
`$860M` favorable reserve development, `47.0` days claims payable, and `$26.5B`
IBNR medical costs payable; Cigna's `84.5%` medical-cost ratio and `$5.09B`
net medical costs payable; and DaVita's `$490M` Q2 operating cash, `$256M`
disclosed FCF, and `7.2266M` treatments. The work separates reserve timing,
claims liabilities, reimbursement, treatment volume, maintenance/development
capital, noncontrolling interests, labor, and legal-entity cash. It improves
the healthcare evidence chain without promoting payer or treatment scale to
normalized owner cash. This is D-122.

The care-delivery refresh now adds primary-source alternate-site and home-care
controls. Option Care reported `$1.442B` Q2 revenue, `$184M` Q2 operating cash,
`$171.485M` H1 operating cash, `$37.941M` H1 receivable use, and `$170.545M`
H1 repurchases. Addus reported `$377.417M` Q2 service revenue, `$40.0M` Q2
operating cash, `264` offices in `24` states, and `32.0%` H1 gross margin.
These figures make payer collection, clinical labor, inventory, branch density,
buyback funding, and segment mix explicit without proving therapy-level margin,
care quality, reimbursement durability, or common-owner cash. This is D-123.

BrightSpring now completes the current-period complex-patient service control:
Q2 revenue was `$3.873B`, adjusted EBITDA `$206M`, and H1 operating cash
`$166.859M`, alongside `$42.203M` acquisitions, `$75.5M` net interest expense,
`$23.169M` share-based compensation, and `$62.909M` of Adjusted EBITDA
adjustments. The March Community Living divestiture makes continuing-operations
comparability an explicit perimeter test. Pharmacy/provider mix, acquisition,
leverage, labor, SBC, and divestiture claims remain separate from common-owner
cash. This is D-124.

The PBF refinancing route now has a new primary-source liquidity boundary. The
Q2 2026 10-Q confirms the June 25 redemption and reports more than `$3.5B` of
operational liquidity after closing, including more than `$800M` cash and
approximately `$2.7B` of revolver availability. This upgrades the period-
matched liquidity test, while trustee settlement, accrued-interest dollars,
cash-on-hand source priority, detailed fees/tax, borrowing-base composition,
and refinery-level return remain open. Operational liquidity is not promoted
to refinancing NPV or common-owner cash. This is D-125.

The BHP FY2026 20-F now closes the recipient-side upfront receipt boundary for
Q-03: BHP records completion of the Wheaton stream, `$4.3B` of upfront
consideration received, and inclusion of the proceeds in net financing
cash-flow analysis. The transaction source/use and issuer-period
classification are therefore stronger than announced terms, while recurring
BHP-PMPA metal credits, sale/receivable, bank collection, tax, and
facility-allocation proof remain open. The upfront proceeds are not promoted to
recurring Antamina owner cash. This is D-126.

The public-use allocation pass now separates BHP's `$4.3B` Antamina receipt
from the approximately `$4.8B` combined realization reported across Antamina,
Carajás, and earlier divestment cash. BHP's FY2026 net-debt outcome and
capital-allocation language do not identify the Antamina share of debt
repayment, growth capital, tax, dividends, or legal-entity residual cash. The
public route is therefore searched-negative for transaction-level use
allocation, and the next source must be a named cash ledger or payment record.
This is D-127.

The TJX cash-flow classification pass now adds a same-period denominator
control: the filing places settlement proceeds in operating activities, but the
`$390M` decrease in prepaid and other current assets is mixed and cannot be
treated as gross settlement cash. The H1 OCF bridge must therefore preserve
the `$419M` net accounting gain, working-capital movement, legal expense, tax,
and maintenance-capital questions as separate objects. This is D-128.

The Walmart Q2 capex/refund pass now adds two cohort controls: the `$2.9B`
tariff refund is a realized gain-contingency event recorded against cost of
sales, and Walmart changed certain segment-overhead allocations in February
2026 while warning that segment results are not directly comparable across
formats and channels. These controls block recurring-margin and pooled-service
denominator claims. This is D-130.

The Enhabit care-delivery pass now records the public-company boundary: its
last public Q1 filing shows `$35.2M` OCF, `$(2.6M)` investing cash,
`$(26.6M)` financing cash, a `$17.7M` non-recurring attorney-fee/mitigation
settlement gain, payer-dependent revenue, and separate Home Health/Hospice
labor economics. The May 15 Kinderhook closing means no public Q2 series is
assumed; post-close payer, labor, debt, capex, and sponsor claims remain
private-source gates. This is D-131.

The Enhabit route now adds the CMS FY2027 hospice reimbursement breaker: a
`2.3%` payment update, `$36,174.75` aggregate cap, `1.7%` quality-reporting
penalty, and nine-measure service/spending variation index. These are payer,
quality, and compliance inputs—not Enhabit-specific cash, patient mix, labor
productivity, or post-close collection proof. This is D-132.

The shared QoE and cross-sector cash-quality panels now carry Enhabit as a
distinct healthcare private-transition lane. They preserve its final public
Q1 cash baseline, payer/labor economics, settlement and refinancing effects,
and the May 15 private-company boundary. The panels route post-close payer,
debt, capex, sponsor, and legal-entity evidence without pooling Enhabit with
UnitedHealth, Cigna, dialysis, or retail denominators. This is D-133.

The end-to-end consistency pass reconciles the prose count with the
authoritative deliverable CSV and verifier: the checked system has `90`
deliverables, not the stale `74` text previously carried in this checkpoint.
This is D-134; it changes documentation consistency only and does not change
the `12 of 13 proven; CA-06 partial` result.

The shared QoE and cash-quality panels now include URI as an asset-backed
capacity lane. They keep stated liquidity, receivables collateral, equipment
borrowing capacity, fleet purchases, sale proceeds, parent cash, URNA transfer
restrictions, and lifecycle return as separate objects. The missing populated
borrowing-base/NOLV certificate remains a Q-13 gate; capacity and resale are
not promoted to owner cash. This is D-135.

The valuation/liquidity stress matrix now carries URI as a distinct
physical-capacity rental lane. Its valuation object is cohort-level rental
yield, utilization, useful life, resale, and replacement—not consolidated FCF
alone. The H1 bridge of `$3.305B` OCF, `$2.885B` rental/non-rental purchases,
and `$706M` equipment-sale proceeds is retained as reported cash evidence, while
the `$2.999B` stated liquidity, `$2.802B` ABL capacity, `$1.779B` receivables
collateral, borrowing-base eligibility, and URNA transfer restrictions remain
separate funding and owner-availability gates. This is D-136.

The valuation and execution layers now separate PBF's completed June 2026
2028-note redemption from the September 14 conditional notice and September 17
issuance of 2032 exchangeable notes. The still-pending redemption carries a
calculated `$519.690M` principal-plus-premium floor before accrued interest,
while the issued financing reports approximately `$533.6M` of net proceeds and
requires separate dilution analysis. The June source/use and post-redemption
liquidity evidence is not reused as September settlement proof or refinancing
NPV. This is D-137.

The shared QoE and cross-sector cash-quality panels now carry the same PBF
separation: June's completed redemption is a source/use fact, while September's
2032 exchangeable financing is issued but the 2030-note settlement remains
pending, with a `$519.690M` pre-interest floor, fee/tax/ABL questions, and
exchangeable dilution. This keeps the financial-shenanigans prompt tied to a
concrete reconciliation instead of counting issuance proceeds or an unsettled
redemption as recurring project economics. This is D-138.

The [healthcare access valuation/liquidity workbench](combined-investment-research-healthcare-access-valuation-liquidity-workbench-2026-09-17.md)

The new healthcare workbench is now linked from both shared Q2 control panels,

The [ordinary-finance valuation/liquidity workbench](combined-investment-research-ordinary-finance-valuation-liquidity-workbench-2026-09-17.md)

The [consumer-goods valuation/liquidity workbench](combined-investment-research-consumer-goods-valuation-liquidity-workbench-2026-09-17.md)

The [medical-devices valuation/liquidity workbench](combined-investment-research-medical-devices-valuation-liquidity-workbench-2026-09-17.md)

The authoritative deliverable registry now matches the verifier's actual

The full named-verifier sweep now passes across the Q-03, URI, PBF, private-

marketplaces. It tests content and rights returns, streaming contribution,
attendance and per-capita spend, maintenance/growth capacity, corporate costs,
debt, NCI, taxes, dividends, and diluted common residual. This is D-193; the
lane remains qualified, unranked, and owner-cash-open until content, rights,
capacity-renewal, and per-share cash joins are evidenced.

The new [asset-integrity inspection valuation/liquidity workbench](combined-investment-research-asset-integrity-inspection-valuation-liquidity-workbench-2026-09-18.md)
extends the goal into MISTRAS Group, keeping certified inspection, monitoring,

This is D-194; the lane remains qualified, unranked, and owner-cash-open until

The new [talent advisory and professional services valuation/liquidity workbench](combined-investment-research-talent-advisory-professional-services-valuation-liquidity-workbench-2026-09-18.md)
extends the goal into Korn Ferry, keeping executive search, interim capacity,

The new [qualified dispensing and packaging valuation/liquidity workbench](combined-investment-research-qualified-dispensing-packaging-valuation-liquidity-workbench-2026-09-18.md)
extends the goal into AptarGroup, keeping qualified dosing, dispensing, and

The new [crop-protection chemistry valuation/liquidity workbench](combined-investment-research-crop-protection-chemistry-valuation-liquidity-workbench-2026-09-18.md)
extends the goal into FMC, keeping active-ingredient, registration,

The new [distributed resilience power valuation/liquidity workbench](combined-investment-research-distributed-resilience-power-valuation-liquidity-workbench-2026-09-18.md)

The new [institutional research and advisory valuation/liquidity workbench](combined-investment-research-institutional-research-advisory-valuation-liquidity-workbench-2026-09-18.md)
